Review: The new Kindle Colorsoft is... fine.

📅 Oct 31, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<img alt="kindle colorsoft on nightstand with amazon kindle library on screen" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/hero-image.jpg" /><p>The <a href="https://mashable.com/roundup/best-kindles-2026-hands-on-tested-reviewed" target="_blank">best Kindle e-readers</a> are essential for book lovers, and Amazon just released a new Kindle Colorsoft (and the Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition with an aluminum body), along with a <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-kindle-e-reader-2026-review" target="_blank">new basic Kindle</a>, <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/kindle-paperwhite-signature-edition-2026-review" target="_blank">Kindle Paperwhite/Paperwhite Signature Edition</a>, and the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/kindle-click-review" target="_blank">Kindle Click page-turner</a>. Following last year's refresh of the <a href="https://mashable.com/review/kindle-scribe-3rd-generation" target="_blank">Kindle Scribe lineup</a>, that means Amazon has an all-new Kindle family, just in time for the holidays.</p><p>I've been reviewing the Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition for Mashable for the past few weeks. Except for the new aluminum body, fig colorway, and higher storage, it's functionally the same as the base Colorsoft model, so we won't be reviewing them separately.</p><p>If you've been curious about Amazon's color e-readers, then I'd recommend reading my Kindle Colorsoft review first. Unfortunately, while there's a lot that I do love about this device, it doesn't fix the biggest problem &mdash; the color is still very disappointing.</p><div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="kindle colorsoft with watchmen cover on display and the graphic novel in the background" class="w-full" height="1125" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-1.fill.size_2000x1125.v1793390220.jpg" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">That cover is definitely yellow-ish.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <h2>Kindle Colorsoft review: Manage your expectations when it comes to color</h2><p>Now, I want to be clear about one thing: Amazon uses E Ink technology for its e-readers. This distinctive, paper-like digital display is a product of the E Ink Corporation. And the truth is that E Ink just doesn't do color very well. So, the technology is what it is.</p><p>The E Ink color experience on the Kindle Colorsoft is disappointing. That was true with the <a href="https://mashable.com/review/amazon-kindle-colorsoft" target="_blank">original model from 2024</a>, and it hasn't changed with the 2026 model. What has changed is that the Colorsoft now offers true edge-to-edge color, so there are no dimming or lighting issues around the edges.</p><p>But Amazon promises readers something more: "Paper-like color perfect for comics, graphic novels, and more." And that's not what I'm seeing. In fact, some of Amazon's official photography with the Kindle Colorsoft appears to exaggerate the color saturation, at least to my eye. I've never seen color quite that bright in practice.</p><div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="Kindle colorsoft library screen with popular book covers on display" class="w-full" height="1600" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-2.fill.size_2000x1600.v1793390220.jpg" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">I haven't seen colors quite this bright on my review unit, sadly.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Amazon</span> </div> </div> <p>I put the Kindle side-by-side with illustrated books, comic books, and graphic novels I have on my bookshelf, and <em>the difference is very obvious</em>, even when you crank up the vividness and brightness to the limit. There's just no comparison, really.</p><p>Even in comics with more neutral tones and softer colors like the classic Batman comic <em>The Long Halloween</em>, the difference stands out clearly. How important is the color problem? Well, the word "color" is right there in the name &mdash; Kindle Colorsoft. Though, I suppose the emphasis should be on "soft."</p><div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="amazon kindle colorsoft on top of watchmen graphic novel" class="w-full" height="1125" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-12.fill.size_2000x1125.v1793390803.jpg" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">The color difference is obvious.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="kindle colorsoft with watchmen comic panel on display" class="w-full" height="1125" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-3.fill.size_2000x1125.v1793390220.jpg" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">What elder millennial nerd doesn't own a copy of 'Watchmen'?</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <p>I have seen more vibrant and colorful electronic paper technology. Samsung's ePaper does a better job with color, though there are other trade-offs. It takes time for the screen to change to a new image, which isn't an option in an e-reader that demands near-instant page turns. </p><p>In my photos of Samsung's ePaper, you'll notice brighter colors, but there's a reason Samsung reserves this tech for commercial use.</p><div class="mx-auto mt-8 max-w-3xl"> <div class="flex flex-col space-y-4 md:flex-row md:space-y-0 md:space-x-4"> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="samsung epaper technology on display with governor's ball merchandise" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1250" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-4.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" style="width: 100%;" width="1000" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">Samsung's ePaper commercial display technology on display.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="close-up view of samsung epaper commercial display" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1251" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-5.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" width="1000" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">A closer look at Samsung's ePaper commercial display technology.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p>If you want to read comics, magazines, or illustrated books in digital form, a tablet like the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/apple-ipad-mini-8-announced" target="_blank">new iPad Mini</a> or <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-alexa-tablets-announcement" target="_blank">Amazon Alexa tablets</a> would be a better option. (Mashable is in the process of reviewing both of those devices.)</p><p>And you can always go to an actual bookstore.</p><h2>Amazon Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition: What I liked</h2><p>To be clear, there is a lot to love about the Kindle Colorsoft, particularly the Kindle Colorsoft Signature Edition. I love the fig color, which is a close match for the new burgundy iPhone 18 Pro. (Fun fact: An Amazon spokesperson told Mashable the fig color was inspired by the same industry trend report that inspired the burgundy iPhone.)</p><p>The new aluminum body available in the Paperwhite SE and Colorsoft SE is worth the upgrade. They have a similar feel to the <a href="https://mashable.com/review/ipad-air-m4-review-apple" target="_blank">Apple iPad Air</a>, which I also loved. It's also satisfying to see the cover screen artwork and your book covers in color.</p><div class="mx-auto mt-8 max-w-3xl"> <div class="flex flex-col space-y-4 md:flex-row md:space-y-0 md:space-x-4"> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="kindle colorsoft held in front of a bookshelf" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1500" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-10.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" style="width: 100%;" width="1200" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="back of kindle colorsoft held in front of a bookshelf" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1500" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-11.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" width="1200" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p>I also appreciated the ability to zoom in on comic panels and illustrations, which is a great way to appreciate fine details in the artwork.</p><div class="mx-auto mt-8 max-w-3xl"> <div class="flex flex-col space-y-4 md:flex-row md:space-y-0 md:space-x-4"> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="kindle colorsoft with a panel from batman's 'the long halloween' comic on display" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1500" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-6.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" style="width: 100%;" width="1200" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="Close-up view of Batman artwork and dialogue from a comic book" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="1500" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/reviews/06xXRxjJbObyjKr15Giogqg/images-7.fit_lim.size_376x.jpg" width="1200" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">...enhance!</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Timothy Werth / Mashable</span> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p>You can double-click to read comics panel-by-panel on the Colorsoft, rather than page-by-page. Often, this works really well, but for artists who use complicated panels of varying sizes and styles (like you'll find in <em>The Long Halloween</em>), some of the panels fit very awkwardly on the screen. </p><p>The new Kindle Colorsoft also has a lot of the features we loved in other Kindle devices &mdash; a waterproof design, a battery that lasts for weeks, auto-adjusting light. Amazon also moved the power button to the side, which is a smart update. Only the Signature Edition supports wireless charging, and it now has magnetic pogo pins on the back. That means if you're used to using Qi wireless charging accessories with your Kindle, they won't work here. For me, this isn't a problem.</p><p>To sum up &mdash; the Kindle Colorsoft isn't a bad color e-reader. It just suffers from the same problems all color e-readers do. </p><h2>The Kindle Colorsoft: Who it's for</h2><p>If you believe some color is better than no color, or if you're a note-taker who loves to highlight their books, then the Kindle Colorsoft would be a smart upgrade from the basic Kindle. (Pro tip: We <a href="https://mashable.com/review/kindle-scribe-colorsoft" target="_blank">named the Kindle Scribe Colorsoft a Mashable Choice product</a>, and the ability to write on the screen lets you use color in much better ways.) However, the Kobo Libra Colour is retaining its top spot as <a href="https://mashable.com/roundup/best-color-e-readers-2026-tested" target="_blank">our favorite color e-reader</a>.</p><p>The Kindle Colorsoft would also be a smart option for kids in the process of graduating from illustrated kids' books to chapter books. The Colorsoft could help make this transition a bit more inviting, and parents can also get a Kindle Colorsoft Kids Edition for $299.99.</p><div class="flex relative flex-col p-6 max-w-3xl mx-auto $79.99</p></li></ul><h2>2026 Kindle price increases</h2><p>For years, Kindle prices have stayed stable, but in August 2026, they finally got an <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-fire-tv-kindle-echo-price-increase-2026" target="_blank">increase</a>. Every model except the Kindle Scribe received a price increase. We can thank <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/ramageddon-every-tech-price-increase-2026" target="_blank">RAMageddon</a>, as an <a href="https://fortune.com/2026/08/21/exclusive-amazon-quietly-hiked-prices-echo-fire-tv-kindle-eero-significant-increases-memory-costs/" target="_blank">Amazon spokesperson said</a> that the increased prices are a result of "significant increases in memory and storage component costs." Here's a breakdown of all the new Kindle prices</p><div class="raw-embed"> <div style="width: 100%; height: 0px; padding: 61.44% 0px 0px; overflow: hidden;"></div> </div><h2>More 2026 Kindle updates</h2><p>As of May 20, 2026, Amazon no longer lend software updates or provide web services to <a href="https://mashable.com/article/amazon-discontinuing-kindle-1st-gen-support" target="_blank">devices released in 2012</a> or earlier, essentially bricking them. Users will still be able to read currently downloaded books on the device, but to add new books, they will need to connect the device to a computer. This is a huge hit, especially to Libby users who need web service access to read borrowed books. </p><p>The affected Kindles include: </p><ul><li><p>Kindle (1st generation)</p></li><li><p>Kindle (2nd generation)</p></li><li><p>Kindle DX</p></li><li><p>Kindle DX Graphite</p></li><li><p>Kindle Keyboard</p></li><li><p>Kindle 4</p></li><li><p>Kindle Touch</p></li><li><p>Kindle 5</p></li><li><p>Kindle Paperwhite (1st generation)</p></li></ul><p>Owners of these <a href="https://mashable.com/article/amazon-kindle-support-ending-backlash-outrage" target="_blank">Kindle models</a> are obviously upset, as it means they'll need to purchase a new device to <a href="https://mashable.com/article/amazon-kindle-losing-support-what-to-do-with-it" target="_blank">maintain access to all features</a>. While some are considering <a href="https://mashable.com/article/should-you-switch-to-a-kobo-kindle-discontinuation" target="_blank">switching to a different e-reader</a>, like a Kobo, Amazon still makes great e-readers, and it's a testament to the brand that devices that are at least 14 years old are still going strong.</p><h2>Which Kindle is right for you?</h2><p>If you're concerned about buying the newest tech, that's not an issue since, as of October 2026, there are <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-kindle-new-devices-launch-oct-2026" target="_blank">nine new models</a> to shop.</p><p>Before you choose a Kindle, you need to ask one question first: Do you want it to be waterproof? If you prefer reading near a body of water &mdash; whether in the bath, by the pool, or at the beach &mdash; that immediately narrows down your options. Since the Kindle Oasis is no longer available, your waterproof options are the <a href="https://zdcs.link/z3KBWm?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Kindle%20Paperwhite&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI&amp;short_url=z3KBWm&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Kindle Paperwhite</a>, <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/kindle-paperwhite-signature-edition-2026-review" target="_blank">Paperwhite Signature Edition</a>, <a href="https://zdcs.link/zj3rqr?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Colorsoft%20Signature%20Edition&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI&amp;short_url=zj3rqr&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Colorsoft Signature Edition</a>, and <a href="https://zdcs.link/z7GvNV?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Paperwhite%20Kids&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI&amp;short_url=z7GvNV&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Paperwhite Kids</a>.</p><div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="A Kindle and a Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition on top of two books" class="w-full" height="1125" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI/images-9.fill.size_2000x1125.v1793213932.jpg" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">Trying to decide which Kindle is right for you? Above all, you need to decide if you need a waterproof e-reader.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Samantha Mangino / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <p>Beyond water resistance, here are a few other features to consider:</p><ul><li><p><strong>Display size:</strong> The basic Kindle has a 6-inch display, while the largest has a 10.2-inch display.</p></li><li><p><strong>Storage capacity:</strong> Kindles range from 16GB to 32GB of storage capacity.</p></li><li><p><strong>Battery life:</strong> The new Kindles generally have outstanding batteries. On the low end, they last up to six weeks per charge, while others last up to 12 weeks.</p></li><li><p><strong>Writing capability: </strong>The Kindle Scribe is the only model that allows you to write with the included stylus.&nbsp;</p></li></ul><p>The latest Kindles all boast a maximum brightness of 94 nits and a 300 ppi, high-resolution display with zero glare, so you can enjoy sharp text and images at any viewing angle. Kindles are also equipped with WiFi connectivity, allowing you to download all the reading material you could ever want. Plus, you can listen to audiobooks on Audible via the built-in Bluetooth connection.</p><p>Not to mention, out of all the e-readers I've tested, Kindles have the best compatibility with Libby. I love the digital platform because I can read for free and support my local library. Some other e-readers require you to download your library e-book to a computer and then upload it to an e-reader, but with Kindles, your e-books are sent straight to your device with a click.</p><p>And if you were a die-hard Kindle Oasis fan and are attached to page-turning buttons, your best option is to get the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition with the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/kindle-page-turn-cover-review" target="_blank">Page Turn Cover</a>.</p><div class="flex mx-auto mt-8 w-full max-w-3xl font-sans text-lg leading-normal md:text-xl md:leading-7"> <span class="font-bold text-primary-400">SEE ALSO:</span> <a class="flex items-center text-secondary-300" href="https://mashable.com/roundup/best-e-readers"> <span class="ml-1">I read over 50 books a year. Here are my favorite e-readers.</span> <svg class="ml-1 w-4 h-4 font-normal fill-current" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg"><use></use></svg> </a> </div> <h2>How to get free e-books on Kindle</h2><div class="mx-auto mt-8 max-w-3xl"> <div class="flex flex-col space-y-4 md:flex-row md:space-y-0 md:space-x-4"> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="A screenshot of a Libby app being downloaded on Kindle" class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="2095" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI/images-5.fit_lim.size_376x.png" style="width: 100%;" width="1179" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">With just a click, transfer your Libby e-book to your Kindle device.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Samantha Mangino / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <div class="w-full md:w-1/2"> <img alt="A screenshot of a Libby book being downloaded into the Kindle library." class="w-full border border-gray-100" height="2095" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/075BHRyIknVFjS6g55iHeyI/images-6.fit_lim.size_376x.png" width="1179" /> <div class="mt-4 font-sans text-left subtitle-2"> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">Your Libby ebook will automatically populate in your Kindle library.</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Samantha Mangino / Mashable</span> </div> </div> </div> </div> <p>The best way to read free e-books on a Kindle is with Libby. <a href="https://mashable.com/article/libby-app-how-to-free-kindle-e-books" target="_blank">Libby</a> allows you to borrow e-books through libraries, and all you need is a library card. Through the Libby app, borrow books and then select the "Read with Kindle" option, which allows you to send these e-books to your Kindle library.</p><p>Another option for finding free e-books is through <a href="https://mashable.com/article/april-22-stuff-your-kindle-day" target="_blank">Stuff Your Kindle events</a>. These are author-driven events that offer limited-time deals on e-books, often making them heavily discounted or totally free.</p><h2>How to make the most of your Kindle</h2><p>Through our testing and daily use, we've spent a lot of time with Kindles, learning how to make the most of them. We've written extensively on all the ways to maximize your Kindle, so be sure to browse through our guides and explainers:</p><ul><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/kindle-hacks-features-to-use?test_uuid=04wb5avZVbBe1OWK6996faM&amp;test_variant=a" target="_blank">How to make the most of your Kindle: 8 features you need to use</a></p></li><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/best-kindle-accessories" target="_blank">The best Kindle accessories for reading in luxury</a></p></li><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/what-is-stuff-your-kindle-day" target="_blank">What is Stuff Your Kindle Day? The best way to fill your e-reader with free books</a></p></li><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/libby-app-how-to-free-kindle-e-books?test_uuid=003aGE6xTMbhuvdzpnH5X4Q&amp;test_variant=b" target="_blank">I've read hundreds of free Kindle books with Libby. Here's how.</a></p></li><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/kindle-rewards" target="_blank">What is Kindle Rewards? Earn free credits when shopping the Kindle store</a></p></li><li><p><a href="https://mashable.com/article/what-is-kindle-unlimited" target="_blank">What is Kindle+? The 10 best e-readers weve ever tested — including the 2026 Kindles

📅 Oct 31, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<img alt="Four e-readers on a table" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl/hero-image.jpg" /><p>So, what is the best e-reader for 2026? I'm regularly testing new e-readers (and <a href="https://mashable.com/article/how-to-turn-tablet-into-e-reader" target="_blank">tablets</a>) and cycling through different models to stay up to date on the best options available. If you're gearing up to read more books this year, I can recommend a model you'll love.</p><p>But what's the e-reader? Well, Amazon just released my new favorite e-reader.</p><div class="mx-auto mt-8 mb-12 w-full max-w-3xl font-sans md:mt-12 md:mb-16 text-primary-400" id="related-video"> <div class="flex flex-row justify-center text-lg text-center md:text-xl"> <hr class="mx-auto border border-gray-100" /> <strong class="py-4 mx-4 w-full min-w-max text-2xl">Featured Video For You</strong> <hr class="mx-auto border border-gray-100" /> </div> <div class="mx-auto mt-5 max-w-2xl"> <div id="video-container-01M68PEQ3V8HFBQPE9095QWG80"></div> <div class="mx-auto mt-8 w-full max-w-3xl text-lg font-semibold leading-5 md:leading-6" id="video-title-container-01M68PEQ3V8HFBQPE9095QWG80"></div> </div> <hr class="mx-auto w-full border border-gray-100" /> </div> <h2>Latest e-reader news</h2><ul><li><p>On Oct. 1, Amazon dropped a redesigned <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-kindle-new-devices-launch-oct-2026" target="_blank">collection of Kindles.</a> We've tested the latest e-readers and named the Kindle Paperwhite Signature Edition the best overall e-reader.</p></li><li><p>As of September 2026, we've added the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/boox-go-6-gen-2-e-reader-tablet-review" target="_blank">Boox Go 6</a> to this guide, naming it the best Kindle alternative. We also named the Kindle Paperwhite the best e-reader for the beach (in addition to being the best overall) and named the Durobo Krono the best e-reader for audiobooks.</p></li><li><p>In August 2026, <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/amazon-fire-tv-kindle-echo-price-increase-2026" target="_blank">Amazon</a> increased the price of Kindles. Now the basic Kindle costs $149.99 ($40 increase) and the Kindle Paperwhite costs $199.99 ($40 increase).</p></li><li><p>In July 2026, Boox announced it was releasing a pocket-sized e-reader, the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/boox-picco-mini-e-reader-announcement" target="_blank">Picco</a>. After <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/boox-picco-ifa-2026-rumors" target="_blank">IFA 2026</a>, rumors floated that the Boox Picco would be released in November 2026.</p></li><li><p>Also in July 2026, Xteink dropped the <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/xteink-x4-pro-gen-2-e-reader-launch" target="_blank">X4 Pro</a>, a new tiny e-reader with a touchscreen.</p></li><li><p>As of April 2026, we have included the <a href="https://zdcs.link/9ZAKBn?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Durobo%20Krono&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl&amp;short_url=9ZAKBn&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Durobo Krono</a> and the <a href="https://zdcs.link/aN3WxV?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Xetink%20X4%20e-reader&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl&amp;short_url=aN3WxV&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Xetink X4 e-reader</a> in this guide.</p></li><li><p>In May 2026, <a href="https://mashable.com/article/amazon-discontinuing-kindle-1st-gen-support" target="_blank">Amazon ceased support for e-readers</a> released in 2012 or earlier, which includes the first generation of Kindle and Kindle Paperwhite.</p></li><li><p>Also in May 2026, Kobo announced its <a href="https://mashable.com/tech/rakuten-kobo-storygraph-integration" target="_blank">integration with Storygraph</a>, which syncs your on-device reading progress with the Storygraph app.</p></li></ul><h2>Which are the most affordable e-readers?</h2><div class="raw-embed"> <div style="width: 100%; height: 0px; padding: 75.39% 0px 0px; overflow: hidden;"></div> </div> <p>When picking an e-reader, price is always a consideration. However, almost all e-readers fall into the same price range. Kindle and Kobo e-readers both start around $149.99/$159.99, while the tiny Boox Picco is just $109.99. If you want handwriting capabilities, Kobo and Boox make the most affordable options, as Kindle's Scribes start at $500.</p><h2>Other e-readers we've tested</h2><div class="eloquent-imagery-image"> <div class="flex justify-center"> <img alt="A Kindle Paperwhite, a Nook GlowLight 4 Plus, and a Kindle Scribe" class="w-full" height="1125" src="https://helios-i.mashable.com/imagery/roundups/06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl/images-1.fill.size_2000x1125.v1727284449.png" width="2000" /> </div> <div class="mt-2 subtitle-2 font-sans "> <span class="normal-case text-gray-1000">From left to right: Kindle Paperwhite, Nook GlowLight 4 Plus, Kindle Scribe</span> <span class="text-gray-600 credit">Credit: Samantha Mangino / Mashable</span> </div> </div> <ul><li><p><a href="https://zdcs.link/QKOLVV?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Remarkable%20PaperPro&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl&amp;short_url=QKOLVV&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)"><strong>Remarkable PaperPro</strong></a><strong>:</strong> Whenever you search for e-readers, <a href="https://zdcs.link/z628Zq?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Remarkable%27s&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl&amp;short_url=z628Zq&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)">Remarkable's</a> tablets will always pop up in the search results, but unfortunately, it's hard to obtain ePUB files on the Remarkable. With Remarkable, you can access files within Google Drive, Dropbox, and OneDrive. If you have any locked ePUB files &mdash; like from Libby &mdash; you won't be able to access them on a Remarkable tablet. So, after testing the Remarkable tablet myself, it proved to be a better document reader or digital journal than an e-reader.</p></li><li><p><a href="https://zdcs.link/Qp7rvv?pageview_type=RSS&amp;template=roundup&amp;module=content_body&amp;element=offer&amp;item=text-link&amp;element_label=Onyx%20Boox%20Go%207&amp;object_type=roundup&amp;object_uuid=06WovVm85HUg7WHtZ2S7Ujl&amp;short_url=Qp7rvv&amp;u=https%3A%2F%2Fmashable.com%2Ffeeds%2Frss%2Fall" rel="sponsored" target="_blank" title="(opens in a new window)"><strong>Onyx Boox Go 7</strong></a><strong>:</strong> While we love the Boox Go 6, we didn't love the Go 7. It's the same size as the Kobo Libra or Amazon Kindle, but it really lags on processing speed. Reading ePUB files from Libby required downloading the Adobe Digital Editions app onto the Boox Go 7, and there was significant lag when opening and closing the app. While it was an easier process than getting library books on a Nook, it was still a major pain compared to Kindle or Kobo's integrations. The Boox Go 7 lagged and took ages to load files. I was most looking forward to testing this Boox because of its page-turning buttons, but unfortunately, I found that they didn't work on my device.</p></li><li><p><strong>Boox Palma 2: </strong>I loved reading on the Boox Palma. It's a really solid palm-sized e-reader with an open Android system, so you can download any reading app from the Google Play store, including Libby, Kindle, and Kobo. Unfortunately, the Palma 2 is being discontinued, and its availability is dwindling, which is why it's not included in this list.</p></li></ul><h2>Where's the Nook on this list?</h2><p>In terms of big-name e-readers, you might notice one omission from this list: the Barnes &amp; Noble Nook. I have tested this e-reader, and it was previously featured on this list. However, after testing more devices, it no longer makes the cut. In comparison to any of the Kindles or the Kobo Libra, the Nook has sloth-like performance, making it frustrating to use.</p><p>Plus, getting my library books onto the Nook was a nightmare, as it was a multi-step process that required downloading to a computer before uploading to a Nook. Its only redeeming quality was the buttons on either side of the device, which made page turning a breeze. However, unless you are a loyal buyer of Barnes &amp; Noble books, I recommend steering clear of the Nook.</p><h2>Where to get e-reader books for free</h2><p>Once you get an e-reader, it's time to fill it up with books, and that doesn't necessarily mean spending money. In fact, you can load up your e-reader without spending a dime; you just need to be a bit resourceful.</p><p>The best way to read nearly any book &mdash; a classic or hot new release &mdash; is with <a href="https://mashable.com/article/libby-app-how-to-free-kindle-e-books" target="_blank">Libby</a>. I have read hundreds of free books with the Libby app &mdash; I wouldn't have been able to test for this guide without it. It connects with your library card, allowing you to borrow from your local library's digital collection. Plus, both Kobo and Kindle have exceptional Libby integrations.</p><p>Another way to build your library is through <a href="https://mashable.com/article/what-is-stuff-your-kindle-day" target="_blank">Stuff Your Kindle Day</a> events. These are 24-hour opportunities led by authors that make books discounted or free.</p><p>If you're a fan of the classics or looking to discover a treasure lost to time, an online resource like Project Gutenberg has more than 75,000 books in the public domain that you can download and read on an e-reader.</p>

One League, Two Sponsorship Rulebooks

📅 Oct 31, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<!-- Article Start --><p>Lazio’s Polymarket shirt deal didn’t survive the summer. In August, the Rome club parted with the prediction-market company after Italian regulators blocked its website. Yet Serie A had signed its own Polymarket agreement only months earlier, and that partnership was aimed squarely at the United States.</p><p>The same company could be ruled out on a Lazio shirt in Italy while appearing in the league’s American media. The difference lies in where each deal is meant to operate.</p><h1>Lazio’s Search for a Shirt Sponsor</h1><figure class="wp-caption alignnone" id="attachment_984602" style="width: 300px;"><img alt="REGGIO NELL'EMILIA, ITALY - OCTOBER 27: Tijjani Noslin of S.S. Lazio celebrates scoring his team’s fifth goal during the Serie A match between US Sassuolo and SS Lazio at Mapei Stadium - Citta' del Tricolore on October 27, 2026 in Reggio nell'Emilia, Italy. (Photo by Emmanuele Ciancaglini/Getty Images)" class="size-medium wp-image-984602" height="200" src="https://icdn.football-italia.net/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Tijjani-Noslin-celebrate-414x276.jpg" width="300" /><figcaption class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-984602">REGGIO NELL’EMILIA, ITALY – OCTOBER 27: Tijjani Noslin of S.S. Lazio celebrates scoring his team’s fifth goal during the Serie A match between US Sassuolo and SS Lazio at Mapei Stadium – Citta’ del Tricolore on October 27, 2026 in Reggio nell’Emilia, Italy. (Photo by Emmanuele Ciancaglini/Getty Images)</figcaption></figure><p>Lazio agreed a two-season sponsorship with Polymarket in April 2026, reportedly worth around €19 million, with an option for another year. It was a significant deal for a club that had struggled to fill the front of its shirt.</p><p>A March breakdown of <a href="https://football-italia.net/serie-a-shirt-sponsors-which-clubs-milan-inter/">Serie A shirt sponsorship earnings</a> identified Lazio as the only club in the division without a main shirt partner. Inter and Milan, by comparison, were each receiving around €30 million annually from their respective arrangements.</p><p>By July, the optimism had faded. Italy’s Customs and Monopolies Agency, known as ADM, had again blocked access to the platform, treating it as an unauthorised gambling operator. Keeping its branding on Lazio shirts had become difficult to reconcile with the Italian advertising rules.</p><h1>Why an American Deal Is Different</h1><figure class="wp-caption alignnone" id="attachment_928003" style="width: 300px;"><img alt="FLORENCE, ITALY - NOVEMBER 22: General view inside the stadium Artemio Franchi during the Serie A match between ACF Fiorentina and Juventus FC at Artemio Franchi on November 22, 2025 in Florence, Italy. (Photo by Gabriele Maltinti/Getty Images)" class="size-medium wp-image-928003" height="201" src="https://icdn.football-italia.net/wp-content/uploads/2025/11/Serie-A-logo-414x277.jpg" width="300" /><figcaption class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-928003">FLORENCE, ITALY – NOVEMBER 22: General view inside the stadium Artemio Franchi during the Serie A match between ACF Fiorentina and Juventus FC at Artemio Franchi on November 22, 2025 in Florence, Italy. (Photo by Gabriele Maltinti/Getty Images)</figcaption></figure><p>Italy’s Dignity Decree restricts advertising for gambling and betting with cash winnings, including sports sponsorship. Whether a prediction market belongs in that category has been central to the Polymarket dispute. The company’s description of its product as a prediction exchange didn’t prevent ADM from treating its Italian activities as unauthorised gambling.</p><p>The United States has a separate regulatory system, and access to prediction markets can depend on the state and the product offered. Readers can see how these distinctions work in practice <a href="https://www.cbssports.com/prediction/news/polymarket-promo-code/" rel="noopener" target="_blank">at CBS Sports Polymarket review</a>, which outlines US eligibility requirements, location restrictions and promotional terms. Those conditions explain who may use the American platform, not whether Polymarket’s branding is permitted on a Serie A shirt in Italy.</p><p>Italian clubs need more than assurances about a sponsor’s US operations. They also need to know whether the branding they sell is permitted at home.</p><h1>Serie A’s US Partnership Has Its Own Terms</h1><p>On 14 May 2026, Serie A USA named Polymarket its exclusive prediction-market partner in the United States under a multi-year arrangement. According to <a href="https://en.legaseriea.it/news/polymarket-named-official-prediction-market-partner-of-serie-a-in-the-u-s" rel="noopener" target="_blank">the league’s official announcement</a>, the agreement covers US media and digital branding, alongside prediction markets using official Serie A data supplied through Genius Sports.</p><p>Those are quite different commercial rights from putting a company logo on a Lazio shirt worn in Italian domestic competition. The league’s deal was expressly regional, aimed at American supporters rather than Italian customers.</p><p>The agreements moved in different directions over the summer:</p><ul> <li>14 May: Serie A USA announced its American partnership with Polymarket.</li> <li>July: Italian restrictions again put Lazio’s shirt deal in doubt.</li> <li>11 August: Lazio and Polymarket agreed to end their sponsorship.</li> </ul><p>Lazio’s announcement concerned the club’s contract. It didn’t say that Serie A USA’s separate agreement had been cancelled.</p><h1>Lazio Secured Payment Despite the Split</h1><figure class="wp-caption alignnone" id="attachment_819404" style="width: 300px;"><img alt="ROME, ITALY - JULY 08: SS Lazio President Claudio Lotito attends a press conference at the Formello Sport Centre on July 08, 2024 in Rome, Italy. (Photo by Marco Rosi - SS Lazio/Getty Images)" class="size-medium wp-image-819404" height="200" src="https://icdn.football-italia.net/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/ss-lazio-unveil-new-coach-marco-baroni-414x276.jpg" width="300" /><figcaption class="wp-caption-text" id="caption-attachment-819404">Lazio President Claudio Lotito (Photo by Marco Rosi – SS Lazio/Getty Images)</figcaption></figure><p>There was an important financial detail in the 11 August settlement. Lazio said Polymarket would pay the full amount stipulated for the 2026/27 season, even though the sponsorship was ending early. Reports valued that season’s portion at approximately €9.5 million.</p><p>That protects one season’s fee, but the shirt space still needs a new buyer for future campaigns. The club and Polymarket also left open the possibility of working together again if the regulatory position changes.</p><h1>A Contract Must Work Where the Football Is Played</h1><p>Serie A clubs are looking beyond Italy for new commercial income, particularly in the American market. Regional media partnerships allow the league to reach that audience without necessarily placing the same branding in Italian stadiums.</p><p>Lazio’s experience shows why those distinctions need to be written into agreements from the outset. A sponsor can have an international profile and still encounter a local restriction serious enough to end a shirt deal. The league may continue to pursue US partnerships, but clubs selling sponsorship space in Italy have to account for the rules governing that space.</p><!-- Article End -->

Backbone of a beast – charting Nvidia's rise to networking titan

📅 Oct 31, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

<p> Nvidia is more than just a leader in graphics and AI hardware. It has also become a titan of the networking industry with bigger datacenter Ethernet sales than any other vendor.</p><p>In Q2, Nvidia's networking revenues <a href="https://www.theregister.com/systems/2026/08/31/nvidia-is-building-an-ip-licensing-empire-on-the-back-of-nvlink/5293530" target="_blank">topped</a> $17.5 billion. To put that in perspective, Cisco's revenue during that same period, across all products, was $17.3 billion.</p><p>GPUzilla's arrival as a networking power feels sudden, yet Jensen Huang started working towards this status in 2019 with the $6.9 billion <a href="https://www.theregister.com/on-prem/2020/05/05/nvidias-multi-billion-dollar-buying-spree-continues-as-it-slurps-up-cumulus-soon-after-swallowing-mellanox/1216486" target="_blank">acquisition</a> of long-time networking partner Mellanox .</p><p>At the time, Mellanox wasn't a big name in Ethernet switching. The company was mostly known for its ConnectX NICs and InfiniBand switches. Outside of high-performance computing (HPC) and supercomputing clusters, and a big deployment at Microsoft, InfiniBand wasn't widely deployed in hyperscale datacenters.</p><p>Buying Mellanox allowed Nvidia to reposition itself as a system builder, which means something different in the context of distributed compute. Nvidia understood its datacenter customers weren't interested in buying GPUs or even servers; they wanted clusters of systems. For that, Nvidia needed interconnect and Mellanox's InfiniBand fit the bill.</p><h3>Relentless co-design</h3><p>Over the years, Huang has talked about co-design – designing components and software to work together for specific set of jobs rather than to be the best GPU, switch, or NIC. Its work with Mellanox is a prime example of that approach.</p><p>According to Dell'Oro Group analyst Sameh Boujelbene, Nvidia's decision to integrate compute and networking became key to the company's success.</p><p>"Customers choose Nvidia for its tightly integrated compute and networking architecture, optimized for AI workloads," she told <span class="italic m-italic">The Register</span>. That integration makes deployments easier and speeds time to market.</p><p>Ben Mabey, CTO of pioneering AI and machine learning company Recursion, told <span class="italic m-italic ">The Reg </span><span>Nvidia's integration schemes won him over.</span></p><p>Founded in 2013, nearly a decade before ChatGPT made AI the subject of dinner-table discussion, Recursion sought to apply machine learning running atop GPUs to the problem of drug discovery.</p><p>This work involved churning through massive datasets which, Mabey tells us, now total more than 50 petabytes in size.</p><p>"When you think about our workloads, it makes LLMs workload training seem easy compared to the amount of data that has to be moved around," he told <span class="italic m-italic ">The Register</span>.</p><p>"In the biological space and sciences in general, it's not well appreciated just how big the data actually is," he added. "The internet's big, but in the grand scheme of things, the text that people are training on is actually pretty small, versus the petabytes of data that we have of these images."</p><p>Recursion's first cluster was a system called BioHive Zero which it cobbled together from GTX 1080 graphics cards originally intended for gaming systems. It used Ethernet to connect GPUs and ran into the limits of conventional networking. </p><p>So, for its second cluster, an A100-based machine with around 300 accelerators, Recursion went with Nvidia's Quantum InfiniBand switches and ConnectX NICs.</p><p>As Mabey explains it, buying its compute and interconnects from the same vendor vastly simplified optimization and troubleshooting. When Recursion reached out to Nvidia for help tuning the system, they were able to consider it as a single system, rather than discrete compute and networking resources. This level of integration had only previously been offered by boutique supercomputer builders like Cray or Atos (Eviden/Bull/whatever it gets renamed next).</p><h3>A wider Spectrum</h3><p>Nvidia hasn't always listed networking revenue as a single line item in its financials. However, based on CFO commentary and a little back of the napkin math we can fill in the blanks.</p><p>Nvidia's networking revenues have grown steadily since the Mellanox acquisition. At two moments, its revenue shot up suddenly.</p><p>The first came in the 2023 calendar year (FY24) after OpenAI kicked off the AI gold rush with the launch of ChatGPT.</p><p>The second spread across 2024 and 2025, when Nvidia started selling Blackwell-generation GPUs and then started to ship them in large quantities.</p><p>"I see two major inflection points, both coinciding with the Blackwell platform ramp: the introduction of NVLink switches and the accelerating adoption of Spectrum-X Ethernet switches for AI back-end networks, driven by growing deployments of Nvidia GPU clusters and increased attach rates of Nvidia switches, NICs, optics, and cables," Boujelbene said.</p><p>It was around this time that users started deploy to Ethernet switches in large volume across Nvidia clusters. Elon Musk's Colossus supercomputer was one of the earliest examples. </p><p>As we <a href="https://www.theregister.com/on-prem/2024/10/29/xais-100000-h100-colossus-is-glued-together-using-ethernet/418032" target="_blank">reported</a> at the time, the first half of the 200,000-chip H100 and H200-based cluster came online in 2024 and, at the time, was among the largest ever clusters deployed using Nvidia's Spectrum-X Ethernet switches.</p><p>Meta has also made extensive use of Nvidia's Spectrum switches in its own training clusters, including at its million-GPU Prometheus site in New Albany, Ohio.</p><p>There are a couple reasons Nvidia's Spectrum-X offering is doing so well.</p><p>First, through a combination of Spectrum Ethernet switches, superNICs, and some clever software optimizations, Nvidia was able to replicate many of the qualities that made InfiniBand so attractive while also benefiting from significantly higher radix and interoperability.</p><p>Ethernet switches, including Nvidia's own, have historically offered more ports. The higher the radix, the fewer switches are necessary to support a cluster at a given port speed. </p><p>This often gets misconstrued to mean that InfiniBand can't scale beyond clusters of a certain size, which isn't necessarily the case. But for clusters exceeding a certain number of GPUs, Ethernet is often more economical because it requires less equipment while achieving similar utilization.</p><p>That said, Nvidia's Spectrum-X isn't exactly unique. Broadcom, Cisco, AMD and others have managed to <a href="https://www.theregister.com/on-prem/2023/05/31/broadcom-says-nvidias-spectrum-x-isnt-that-special/1364562" target="_blank">achieve</a> utilization similar to InfiniBand through various investments in smartNICs and switches.</p><p>Nvidia's main differentiator, as Mabey and Boujelbene noted earlier, is the level of integration between the compute and networking.</p><h3>The next frontier</h3><p>Then Blackwell hit the market, enabling much greater scale and creating a surge in Nvidia's networking revenue.</p><p>Prior to Blackwell, Nvidia's NVLink and NVSwitch interconnect tech had largely been limited to single systems with up to eight GPUs. With the launch of the GB200 NVL72, these NVSwitches were broken out into dedicated appliances. (And yes, we remember GH200 NVL256 and NVL32, but from what we can tell these were never shipped in any meaningful volume, if at all.)</p><p>For every <a href="https://www.theregister.com/on-prem/2024/03/21/a-closer-look-at-nvidias-120kw-dgx-gb200-nvl72-rack-system/912087" target="_blank">NVL72 rack</a> system sold, Nvidia also sold nine NVSwitch trays, which enabled the system's six dozen GPUs to function as a single enormous GPU. This effectively guaranteed steady networking revenue growth: even if customers didn't buy Nvidia's scale-out switches, if they wanted NVL72 racks, they were automatically buying NVSwitch.</p><p>"We estimate that NVSwitch now accounts for roughly one-third of Nvidia’s networking revenue, up from virtually zero less than two years ago," Boujelbene told <span class="italic m-italic ">El Reg</span>. "This underscores how quickly NVLink switching has become a major growth engine for Nvidia’s networking business."</p><p>Nvidia is now moving to grow its networking business even further with a new licensing program.</p><p>Until recently, Nvidia's NVSwitch tech was limited to its own accelerators. However, with the introduction of NVLink Fusion early last year, Nvidia opened the interconnect tech up to <a href="https://www.theregister.com/systems/2026/08/31/nvidia-is-building-an-ip-licensing-empire-on-the-back-of-nvlink/5293530" target="_blank">third parties to license</a>.</p><p>Many chip designers, including Marvell, Qualcomm, Arm, Fujitsu, MediaTek, and d-Matrix, have announced plans to develop CPUs and XPUs using NVLink Fusion. The adoption of the interconnect opens the door to additional NVSwitch sales, as the chips using NVLink Fusion still rely on them for chip-to-chip communications, and there's no other place to get them, as Nvidia has yet to license out NVSwitch.</p><p>"NVLink Fusion allows Nvidia to extend its networking footprint beyond its own GPUs into the rapidly growing custom accelerator market. I see it as a strategic move to capture a larger share of AI networking spending, even in clusters that do not use Nvidia GPUs," Boujelbene said.</p><p>Nvidia's networking ambitions don't stop there.</p><p>"Nvidia continues to pursue opportunities in emerging networking domains as network requirements evolve to keep pace with increasingly demanding AI workloads," Boujelbene added. "The latest example is scale-in networking, designed to address the new requirements of agentic AI, while innovations such as CPO will help overcome growing bandwidth, power, and scalability challenges." ®</p>
